class="mergedrow"><td colspan="2" class="infobox-full-data"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/></td></tr><tr class="mergedrow"><th scope="row" </td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Legislature</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/General_Fono" title="General Fono">General <i>Fono</i></a></td></tr><tr <p><b>Tokelau</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;t&#39; in &#39;tie&#39;">t</span><span title="/oʊ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;code&#39;">oʊ</span><span title="&#39;k&#39; in &#39;kind&#39;">k</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="/aʊ/: &#39;ou&#39; in &#39;mouth&#39;">aʊ</span></span>/</a></span></span>; <abbr style="font-size:85%" title="literal translation">lit.</abbr><span style="white-space: nowrap;">&#8201;</span>'north-northeast&#39;&#32; or 'north wind&#39;;<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> known previously as the <b>Union Islands</b>, and, until 1976, known officially as the <b>Tokelau Islands</b><sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>) is a <a href="/wiki/Dependent_territory" title="Dependent territory">dependent territory</a> of <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand" title="New Zealand">New Zealand</a> in the southern Pacific Ocean. It consists of three tropical coral <a href="/wiki/Atoll" title="Atoll">atolls</a>: <a href="/wiki/Atafu" title="Atafu">Atafu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nukunonu" title="Nukunonu">Nukunonu</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Fakaofo" title="Fakaofo">Fakaofo</a>. They have a combined land area of 10&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (4&#160;sq&#160;mi). The capital rotates yearly among the three atolls.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> In addition to these three, <a href="/wiki/Swains_Island" title="Swains Island">Swains Island</a>, which forms part of the same <a href="/wiki/Archipelago" title="Archipelago">archipelago</a>, is the subject of an ongoing territorial dispute; it is currently administered by the United States as part of <a href="/wiki/American_Samoa" title="American Samoa">American Samoa</a>. Tokelau lies north of the <a href="/wiki/Samoan_Islands" title="Samoan Islands">Samoan Islands</a>, east of <a href="/wiki/Tuvalu" title="Tuvalu">Tuvalu</a>, south of the <a href="/wiki/Phoenix_Islands" title="Phoenix Islands">Phoenix Islands</a>, southwest of the more distant <a href="/wiki/Line_Islands" title="Line Islands">Line Islands</a>, and northwest of the <a href="/wiki/Cook_Islands" title="Cook Islands">Cook Islands</a>.
</p><p>Tokelau has a population of approximately 1,500 people; it has the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_population#Sovereign_states_and_dependencies_by_population" title="List of countries and dependencies by population">fourth-smallest population</a> of any sovereign state or dependency in the world. As of the 2016 census, around 45% of its residents <a href="/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states_and_dependent_territories_by_immigrant_population" title="List of sovereign states and dependent territories by immigrant population">had been born overseas</a>, mostly in Samoa or New Zealand.<sup id="cite_ref-tokelau.org.nz_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tokelau.org.nz-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> The populace has a <a href="/wiki/Life_expectancy" title="Life expectancy">life expectancy</a> of 69, which is comparable to that of other <a href="/wiki/Oceania" title="Oceania">Oceanian</a> island nations. Approximately 94% of the population speak <a href="/wiki/Tokelauan_language" title="Tokelauan language">Tokelauan</a> as their first language. Tokelau has the smallest economy of any nation, although it is a leader in <a href="/wiki/Renewable_energy" title="Renewable energy">renewable energy</a>, being the first 100% <a href="/wiki/Solar-power" class="mw-redirect" title="Solar-power">solar-powered</a> nation in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated1_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Tokelau is officially referred to as a <i><a href="/wiki/Nation" title="Nation">nation</a></i> by both the New Zealand government and the Tokelauan government.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated1_11-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> It is a free and democratic nation with <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Tokelau" title="Politics of Tokelau">elections every three years</a>. However, in 2007, the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly" title="United Nations General Assembly">United Nations General Assembly</a> included Tokelau on its <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_list_of_non-self-governing_territories" title="United Nations list of non-self-governing territories">list of non-self-governing territories</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> Its inclusion on this list is controversial, as Tokelauans have twice narrowly voted against further <a href="/wiki/Self-determination" title="Self-determination">self-determination</a>, and the islands' small population makes the viability of self-government challenging. The basis of Tokelau's legislative, administrative and judicial systems is the <a href="/wiki/Tokelau_Islands_Act_1948" class="mw-redirect" title="Tokelau Islands Act 1948">Tokelau Islands Act 1948</a>, which has been amended several times. Since 1993, the territory has annually elected its own head of government, the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_Government_of_Tokelau" title="Head of Government of Tokelau"><i>Ulu-o-Tokelau</i></a>. Before 1993, the <a href="/wiki/Administrator_of_Tokelau" title="Administrator of Tokelau">administrator of Tokelau</a> was the highest official in the government and the territory was directly administered by a New Zealand government department.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology">Etymology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Etymology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p><span title="Tokelauan-language text"><i lang="tkl">Tokelau</i></span> is a word meaning "north wind" in the native <a href="/wiki/Tokelau_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Tokelau language">Tokelau language</a>. The Tokelau islands were named the <i>Union Islands</i> and <i>Union Group</i> by European explorers at an earlier time.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> <i>Tokelau Islands</i> was adopted as the islands’ official name in 1946. The name was officially shortened to <i>Tokelau</i> on 9 December 1976.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/British_Western_Pacific_Territories" title="British Western Pacific Territories">British Western Pacific Territories</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Pre-history">Pre-history</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Pre-history">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Archaeological evidence indicates that the atolls of Tokelau – <a href="/wiki/Atafu" title="Atafu">Atafu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nukunonu" title="Nukunonu">Nukunonu</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Fakaofo" title="Fakaofo">Fakaofo</a> – were settled about 1,000 years ago from Samoa and may have been a gateway into Eastern Polynesia.<sup id="cite_ref-Research_Coomons_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Research_Coomons-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> The inhabitants embrace <a href="/wiki/Polynesian_mythology" class="mw-redirect" title="Polynesian mythology">Polynesian mythology</a> and the local god, <a href="/wiki/Tui_Tokelau" title="Tui Tokelau">Tui Tokelau</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-SSP1_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SSP1-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> Over time, they developed distinctive <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Tokelau" title="Music of Tokelau">musical</a> and art forms. The three atolls have historically functioned separately politically, while maintaining social and linguistic cohesion. Tokelauan society has been governed by chiefly <a href="/wiki/Clan" title="Clan">clans</a>, and there have been occasional skirmishes and wars between the atolls, as well as inter-marriage. Fakaofo, the "chiefly island",<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> held some dominion over Atafu and Nukunonu after the dispersal of Atafu. Life on the atolls was historically subsistence-based, with a diet that relied mainly on fish and <a href="/wiki/Coconut" title="Coconut">coconut</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-mfat_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mfat-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Contact_with_other_cultures">Contact with other cultures</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Contact with other cultures">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Bowditch_Tokelau.png" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/64/Bowditch_Tokelau.png/220px-Bowditch_Tokelau.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="161" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/64/Bowditch_Tokelau.png/330px-Bowditch_Tokelau.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/64/Bowditch_Tokelau.png/440px-Bowditch_Tokelau.png 2x" data-file-width="462" data-file-height="339" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Bowditch_Tokelau.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Fakaofo islanders, drawn in 1841 by the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Exploring_Expedition" title="United States Exploring Expedition">United States Exploring Expedition</a></div></div></div>
<p>The first European to sight Atafu was Commodore <a href="/wiki/John_Byron" title="John Byron">John Byron</a>, on 24 June 1765. He called the island "Duke of York's Island". Parties from his expedition who ventured ashore reported that there were no signs of current or previous inhabitants.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-mac30_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mac30-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Edward_Edwards_(Royal_Navy_officer)" title="Edward Edwards (Royal Navy officer)">Captain Edward Edwards</a>, having learned of Byron's discovery, visited Atafu on 6 June 1791<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> in search of the <a href="/wiki/Mutiny_on_the_Bounty" title="Mutiny on the Bounty"><i>Bounty</i> mutineers</a>. They found no inhabitants, but saw that there were houses containing canoes and fishing gear, which suggested to them that the island was being used as a temporary residence by fishing parties from other, nearby islands.<sup id="cite_ref-mac30_22-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mac30-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> On 12 June 1791, Edwards sailed farther south,  and sighted Nukunonu, naming it "Duke of Clarence's Island".<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> A landing party that went ashore was unable to make contact with the inhabitants, but saw "<i>morai</i>s", burying places, and canoes with "stages in their middle" sailing across the island's lagoons.<sup id="cite_ref-mac30_22-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mac30-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>
On 29 October 1825, August R. Strong of the <a href="/wiki/USS_Dolphin_(1821)" title="USS Dolphin (1821)">USS <i>Dolphin</i></a> and his crew arrived at the atoll Nukunonu. He wrote: </p><blockquote><p>Upon examination, we found they had removed all the women and children from the settlement, which was quite small, and put them in canoes lying off a rock in the lagoon. They would frequently come near the shore, but when we approached they would pull off with great noise and precipitation.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>On 14 February 1835, Captain Smith, of the United States <a href="/wiki/Whaling" title="Whaling">whaling ship</a> the <i>General Jackson</i>, wrote of having sighted Fakaofo, which he chose to call "D'Wolf's Island".<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-booklet_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-booklet-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> On 25 January 1841, the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Exploring_Expedition" title="United States Exploring Expedition">United States Exploring Expedition</a> visited Atafu, and discovered a small population living on the island. The residents appeared to be there only temporarily, because there was no chief among them, and they had the kind of double <a href="/wiki/Canoe" title="Canoe">canoes</a> that were typically used for inter-island travel. They appeared to have interacted with foreigners in the past, because they expressed a desire to engage in barter with the expedition crew, and they possessed items that were apparently of foreign origin: blue beads and a plane-iron. A few days later, French explorer Captain Morvan sighted Fakaofo.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> The American expedition reached Nukunonu on 28 January 1841, but did not record any information about inhabitants. On 29 January 1841, the expedition sighted Fakaofo and named it "Bowditch".<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> The Fakaofo islanders were found to be similar in appearance and behavior to the Atafu islanders.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Missionaries preached <a href="/wiki/Christianity_in_Tokelau" class="mw-redirect" title="Christianity in Tokelau">Christianity in Tokelau</a> from 1845 to the 1870s. French <a href="/wiki/Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic">Catholic</a> missionaries on <a href="/wiki/Wallis_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="Wallis Island">Wallis Island</a> (also known as 'Uvea) and missionaries of the <a href="/wiki/Protestant" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestant">Protestant</a> <a href="/wiki/London_Missionary_Society" title="London Missionary Society">London Missionary Society</a> in Samoa used native teachers to convert the Tokelauans. Atafu was converted to Protestantism by the London Missionary Society, Nukunonu was converted to Catholicism and Fakaofo was converted to both denominations.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> The Rev. Samuel James Whitmee, of the <a href="/wiki/London_Missionary_Society" title="London Missionary Society">London Missionary Society</a>, visited Tokelau in 1870.<sup id="cite_ref-JCC_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-JCC-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Helped by <a href="/wiki/Swains_Island" title="Swains Island">Swains Island</a>-based Eli Jennings senior, <a href="/wiki/Peru" title="Peru">Peruvian</a> <a href="/wiki/Blackbirding" title="Blackbirding">"blackbird"</a>  <a href="/wiki/Slave_trader" class="mw-redirect" title="Slave trader">slave traders</a> arrived in 1863 and kidnapped nearly all (253) of the able-bodied men to work as labourers, depopulating the atolls.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> The Tokelauan men died of <a href="/wiki/Dysentery" title="Dysentery">dysentery</a> and <a href="/wiki/Smallpox" title="Smallpox">smallpox</a>, and very few returned. With that loss, the system of governance became based on the "Taupulega", or "Councils of Elders", on which individual families on each atoll were represented.<sup id="cite_ref-mfat_20-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mfat-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-booklet_27-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-booklet-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> During that time, <a href="/wiki/Polynesia" title="Polynesia">Polynesian</a> immigrants settled, followed by American, <a href="/wiki/Scottish_people" title="Scottish people">Scottish</a>, French, <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_people" title="Portuguese people">Portuguese</a><sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> and German <a href="/wiki/Beachcombing#Historical_usage" title="Beachcombing">beachcombers</a>, marrying local women and repopulating the atolls.<sup id="cite_ref-booklet_27-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-booklet-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Between 1856 and 1979, the United States claimed that it held sovereignty over the island and the other Tokelauan atolls. In 1979, the U.S. conceded that Tokelau was under New Zealand sovereignty, and a <a href="/wiki/Maritime_boundary" title="Maritime boundary">maritime boundary</a> between Tokelau and <a href="/wiki/American_Samoa" title="American Samoa">American Samoa</a> was established by the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Tokehega" title="Treaty of Tokehega">Treaty of Tokehega</a>.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Fakaofo_village_square_20070716.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/Fakaofo_village_square_20070716.jpg/220px-Fakaofo_village_square_20070716.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/Fakaofo_village_square_20070716.jpg/330px-Fakaofo_village_square_20070716.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/Fakaofo_village_square_20070716.jpg/440px-Fakaofo_village_square_20070716.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2560" data-file-height="1920" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Fakaofo_village_square_20070716.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The square in the centre of the village of <a href="/wiki/Fakaofo" title="Fakaofo">Fakaofo</a></div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Tropical_cyclones">Tropical cyclones</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Tropical cyclones">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Cyclone_Percy" title="Cyclone Percy">Cyclone Percy</a> struck and severely damaged Tokelau in late February and early March 2005. Forecasters underestimated the cyclone's strength and the length of time it would be in vicinity to Tokelau. It coincided with a spring <a href="/wiki/Tide" title="Tide">tide</a> which put most of the area of the two villages on Fakaofo and Nukunonu under a metre of seawater. The cyclone also caused major <a href="/wiki/Erosion" title="Erosion">erosion</a> on several islets of all three atolls, damaging roads and bridges and disrupting electric power and telecommunications systems. The cyclone did significant and widespread damage to food crops including bananas, <a href="/wiki/Coconut" title="Coconut">coconuts</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pandanus" title="Pandanus">pandanus</a>. It did not seriously injure anyone but villagers lost significant amounts of property.
</p><p>No significant land is more than two metres (6.6 feet) above <a href="/wiki/High_water" class="mw-redirect" title="High water">high water</a> of ordinary tides. This means Tokelau is particularly vulnerable to any possible <a href="/wiki/Sea_level_rise" title="Sea level rise">sea level rises</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Time_zone">Time zone</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Time zone">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Time_in_New_Zealand" title="Time in New Zealand">Time in New Zealand</a></div>
<p>Until December 2011, Tokelau was 11&#160;hours behind <a href="/wiki/Coordinated_Universal_Time" title="Coordinated Universal Time">Coordinated Universal Time</a> (UTC).<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> At midnight 29 December 2011 Tokelau shifted to <a href="/wiki/UTC%2B13:00" title="UTC+13:00">UTC+13:00</a> in response to Samoa's decision to switch sides of the International Dateline.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> This brought Tokelau closer to <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand" title="New Zealand">New Zealand</a> time (and in the process omitted 30 December).<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Many sources<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Words_to_watch#Unsupported_attributions" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Words to watch"><span title="The material near this tag possibly uses too-vague attribution or weasel words. (January 2023)">who?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> claim that Tokelau is 14&#160;hours ahead of UTC (UTC−10 before the 2011 date switch), but the correct time zone offset is UTC+13:00.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government">Government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_history_of_Tokelau" title="Constitutional history of Tokelau">Constitutional history of Tokelau</a></div>
<p>In 1877, the islands were included under the protection of the <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom_of_Great_Britain_and_Ireland" title="United Kingdom of Great Britain and Ireland">United Kingdom</a> by an <a href="/wiki/Order_in_Council" title="Order in Council">Order in Council</a> that claimed jurisdiction over all unclaimed <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="Pacific Islands">Pacific Islands</a>. Commander C. F. Oldham on <a href="/wiki/HMS_Egeria_(1873)" title="HMS Egeria (1873)">HMS <i>Egeria</i></a> landed at each of the three atolls in June 1889<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> and officially raised the <a href="/wiki/Union_Flag" class="mw-redirect" title="Union Flag">Union Flag</a>, declaring the group a British <a href="/wiki/Protectorate" title="Protectorate">protectorate</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Colonial_Law&#39;_1966._P._894_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Colonial_Law&#39;_1966._P._894-40">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> In conformity with desire expressed by "the Native government" they were annexed by the United Kingdom and included in the <a href="/wiki/Gilbert_Islands" title="Gilbert Islands">Gilbert Islands</a> by the Tokelau Islands (Union Islands) Order in Council, 1916.<sup id="cite_ref-Colonial_Law&#39;_1966._P._894_40-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Colonial_Law&#39;_1966._P._894-40">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> The annexation took place on 29 February 1916.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> From the point in time that the islands were annexed, their people had the status of <a href="/wiki/British_subjects" class="mw-redirect" title="British subjects">British subjects</a>. Tokelau was removed from the <a href="/wiki/Gilbert_and_Ellice_Islands_Colony" class="mw-redirect" title="Gilbert and Ellice Islands Colony">Gilbert and Ellice Islands Colony</a> and placed under the jurisdiction of the <a href="/wiki/Governor-General_of_New_Zealand" title="Governor-General of New Zealand">Governor-General of New Zealand</a> in 1925, two Orders in Council being made for the purpose on the same day.<sup id="cite_ref-Colonial_Law&#39;_1966._P._894_40-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Colonial_Law&#39;_1966._P._894-40">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> This step meant that New Zealand took over administration of Tokelau from the British on 11 February 1926.<sup id="cite_ref-ReferenceA_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceA-44">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> At this point, Tokelau was still a territory under the sovereignty of the United Kingdom but administered by New Zealand.<sup id="cite_ref-ReferenceA_44-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceA-44">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Union Islands (Revocation) Order in Council, 1948<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> after reciting the agreement by the governments of the United Kingdom and New Zealand that the islands should become part of New Zealand, revoked the Union Islands (No. 2) Order in Council, 1925, with effect from a date fixed by the Governor-General of New Zealand after he was satisfied that the New Zealand Parliament had provided for the incorporation of the islands with New Zealand, as it did by the <i>Tokelau Islands Act 1948</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> Tokelau formally became part of New Zealand on 1 January 1949.<sup id="cite_ref-ReferenceA_44-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceA-44">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Dominion_of_New_Zealand" title="Dominion of New Zealand">Dominion of New Zealand</a>, of which Tokelau formerly was a part, has since been superseded by the <a href="/wiki/Realm_of_New_Zealand" title="Realm of New Zealand">Realm of New Zealand</a>, of which Tokelau remains a part. Defence is the responsibility of New Zealand. When the <i>British Nationality and New Zealand Citizenship Act 1948</i> came into effect on 1 January 1949, Tokelauans who were British subjects gained <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand_citizenship" class="mw-redirect" title="New Zealand citizenship">New Zealand citizenship</a>; a status they still hold.<sup id="cite_ref-Aliens_and_citizens_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Aliens_and_citizens-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Villages are entitled to enact their own laws regulating their daily lives and New Zealand law only applies where it has been extended by specific enactment. Serious crime is rare and there are no prisons, and offenders are publicly rebuked, fined or made to work.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics">Politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Tokelau" title="Politics of Tokelau">Politics of Tokelau</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a> is <span class="nowrap"><a href="/wiki/Charles_III" title="Charles III">Charles III</a></span>, the <a href="/wiki/Monarchy_in_New_Zealand" class="mw-redirect" title="Monarchy in New Zealand">King in right of New Zealand</a>, who also reigns over the other <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_realm" title="Commonwealth realm">Commonwealth realms</a>. The King is represented in the territory by the <a href="/wiki/Administrator_of_Tokelau" title="Administrator of Tokelau">Administrator</a> – currently Don Higgins. The current head of government is <a href="/wiki/Siopili_Perez" title="Siopili Perez">Siopili Perez</a>, who presides over the <a href="/wiki/Council_for_the_Ongoing_Government_of_Tokelau" title="Council for the Ongoing Government of Tokelau">Council for the Ongoing Government of Tokelau</a>, which functions as a cabinet. The Council consists of the <i>faipule</i> (leader) and <i>pulenuku</i> (village mayor) of each of the three atolls.<sup id="cite_ref-TokelauCouncilGovernance_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TokelauCouncilGovernance-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> The administrator is appointed by the <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Foreign_Affairs_(New_Zealand)" title="Minister of Foreign Affairs (New Zealand)">minister of Foreign Affairs and Trade</a> of New Zealand, and the role of <a href="/wiki/List_of_heads_of_government_of_Tokelau" class="mw-redirect" title="List of heads of government of Tokelau">head of government</a> rotates between the three <i>faipule</i> for a one-year term.<sup id="cite_ref-TokelauCouncilGovernance_49-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TokelauCouncilGovernance-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Tokelau Amendment Act of 1996 confers legislative power on the <a href="/wiki/General_Fono" title="General Fono">General Fono</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Unicameralism" title="Unicameralism">unicameral</a> body. The number of seats each atoll receives in the Fono is determined by population – at present, Fakaofo and Atafu each have seven and Nukunonu has six.<sup id="cite_ref-TokelauCouncilGovernance_49-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TokelauCouncilGovernance-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> <i>Faipule</i> and <i>pulenuku</i> also sit in the Fono.<sup id="cite_ref-TokelauCouncilGovernance_49-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TokelauCouncilGovernance-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 11 November 2004, Tokelau and New Zealand took steps to formulate a treaty that would turn Tokelau from a non-self-governing territory to a self-governing state in <a href="/wiki/Associated_state" title="Associated state">free association</a> with New Zealand. Besides the treaty, a United Nations-sponsored <a href="/wiki/2006_Tokelauan_self-determination_referendum" title="2006 Tokelauan self-determination referendum">referendum on self-determination</a> took place, with the three islands voting on successive days starting 13 February 2006. (Tokelauans in <a href="/wiki/Apia,_Samoa" class="mw-redirect" title="Apia, Samoa">Apia, Samoa</a>, voted on 11 February.)<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Out of 581 votes cast, 349 were for Free Association, being short of the two-thirds majority required for the measure to pass.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> The referendum was profiled (somewhat light-heartedly) in the 1 May 2006 issue of <i><a href="/wiki/The_New_Yorker" title="The New Yorker">The New Yorker</a></i> magazine.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> A repeat <a href="/wiki/2007_Tokelauan_self-determination_referendum" title="2007 Tokelauan self-determination referendum">referendum took place on 20–24 October 2007</a>, again narrowly failing to approve self-government. This time the vote was short by just 16 votes or 3%.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In May 2008, the United Nations' Secretary General <a href="/wiki/Ban_Ki-moon" title="Ban Ki-moon">Ban Ki-moon</a> urged colonial powers "to complete the decolonization process in every one of the remaining 16 <a href="/wiki/Non-self-governing_territories" class="mw-redirect" title="Non-self-governing territories">non-self-governing territories</a>", including Tokelau.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> This led <i><a href="/wiki/The_New_Zealand_Herald" title="The New Zealand Herald">The New Zealand Herald</a></i> to comment that the United Nations was "apparently frustrated by two failed attempts to get Tokelau to vote for independence".<sup id="cite_ref-NZ_Herald_10510595_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NZ_Herald_10510595-55">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> In April 2008, speaking as leader of the <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand_National_Party" title="New Zealand National Party">National Party</a>, future New Zealand Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/John_Key" title="John Key">John Key</a> stated that New Zealand had "imposed two referenda on the people of the Tokelau Islands", and questioned "the accepted wisdom that small states should undergo a de-colonisation process".<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti">.mw-parser-output .tmulti .multiimageinner{display:flex;flex-direction:column}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow{display:flex;flex-direction:row;clear:left;flex-wrap:wrap;width:100%;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .tsingle{margin:1px;float:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .theader{clear:both;font-weight:bold;text-align:center;align-self:center;background-color:transparent;width:100%}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .thumbcaption{background-color:transparent}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .text-align-left{text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .text-align-right{text-align:right}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .text-align-center{text-align:center}@media all and (max-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .tmulti .thumbinner{width:100%!important;box-sizing:border-box;max-width:none!important;align-items:center}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow{justify-content:center}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .tsingle{float:none!important;max-width:100%!important;box-sizing:border-box;text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .tsingle .thumbcaption{text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow>.thumbcaption{text-align:center}}</style><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:204px;max-width:204px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><a href="/wiki/File:Atafu.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Atafu.jpg/200px-Atafu.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="132" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Atafu.jpg/300px-Atafu.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Atafu.jpg/400px-Atafu.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3032" data-file-height="2004" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption">Atafu Atoll</div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><a href="/wiki/File:Nukunonu.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Nukunonu.jpg/200px-Nukunonu.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="301" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Nukunonu.jpg/300px-Nukunonu.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Nukunonu.jpg/400px-Nukunonu.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2032" data-file-height="3060" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption">Nukunonu Atoll</div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><a href="/wiki/File:Fakaofo_Satellite_NASA.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Fakaofo_Satellite_NASA.jpg/200px-Fakaofo_Satellite_NASA.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="132" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Fakaofo_Satellite_NASA.jpg/300px-Fakaofo_Satellite_NASA.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Fakaofo_Satellite_NASA.jpg/400px-Fakaofo_Satellite_NASA.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3032" data-file-height="2004" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption">Fakaofo Atoll</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Tokelau includes three atolls in the South Pacific Ocean between longitudes <a href="/wiki/171st_meridian_west" title="171st meridian west">171° W</a> and <a href="/wiki/173rd_meridian_west" title="173rd meridian west">173° W</a> and between latitudes <a href="/wiki/8th_parallel_south" title="8th parallel south">8° S</a> and <a href="/wiki/10th_parallel_south" title="10th parallel south">10° S</a>, about midway between Hawaii and New Zealand. From Atafu in the north to Fakaofo in the south, Tokelau extends for less than 200&#160;km. The atolls lie about 500 kilometres (311 miles) north of <a href="/wiki/Samoa" title="Samoa">Samoa</a>. The atolls are <a href="/wiki/Atafu" title="Atafu">Atafu</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nukunonu" title="Nukunonu">Nukunonu</a>, both in an island group once called the Duke of Clarence Group, and <a href="/wiki/Fakaofo" title="Fakaofo">Fakaofo</a>, once Bowditch Island. Their combined land area is 10.8&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (4.2&#160;sq&#160;mi). The atolls each have <a href="/wiki/List_of_islands_of_Tokelau" title="List of islands of Tokelau">a number of coral islands</a>, where <a href="/wiki/List_of_villages_in_Tokelau" title="List of villages in Tokelau">the villages</a> are situated. The highest point of Tokelau is just 5 metres (16 feet) <a href="/wiki/Height_above_mean_sea_level" title="Height above mean sea level">above sea level</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> There are no ports or harbours for large vessels, however, all three atolls have a jetty to and from which supplies and passengers are shipped.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> Tokelau lies in the Pacific <a href="/wiki/Tropical_cyclone" title="Tropical cyclone">tropical cyclone</a> belt. A fourth island that is culturally, historically, and geographically, but not politically, a part of the Tokelau Islands is <a href="/wiki/Swains_Island" title="Swains Island">Swains Island</a> (Olohega), under United States control since about 1900 and administered as a part of <a href="/wiki/American_Samoa" title="American Samoa">American Samoa</a> since 1925.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Swains Island was claimed by the United States pursuant to the <a href="/wiki/Guano_Islands_Act" title="Guano Islands Act">Guano Islands Act</a>, as were the other three islands of Tokelau; the latter three claims were ceded to Tokelau by treaty in 1979. In the draft constitution of Tokelau subject to the Tokelauan self-determination referendum in 2006, Olohega (Swains Island) was also claimed as a part of Tokelau, though the claim was surrendered in the same 1979 treaty. This established a clearly defined boundary between American Samoa and Tokelau.
</p><p>Tokelau's claim to Swains is generally comparable to the <a href="/wiki/Marshall_Islands" title="Marshall Islands">Marshall Islands</a>' claim to U.S.-administered <a href="/wiki/Wake_Island" title="Wake Island">Wake Island</a>, but the re-emergence of this somewhat dormant issue has been an unintended result of the United Nations' recent efforts to promote decolonisation in Tokelau. Tokelauans have proven somewhat reluctant to push their national identity in the political realm: recent decolonisation moves have mainly been driven from outside for ideological reasons. But at the same time, Tokelauans are reluctant to disown their common cultural identity with Swains Islanders who speak their language.
</p>

</caption>

</th>
<th>Coordinates
</th></tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="/wiki/Atafu" title="Atafu">Atafu</a>
</td>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Environment">Environment</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Environment">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/List_of_birds_of_Tokelau" title="List of birds of Tokelau">List of birds of Tokelau</a> and <a href="/wiki/List_of_mammals_of_Tokelau" title="List of mammals of Tokelau">List of mammals of Tokelau</a></div>
<p>Tokelau is located in the <a href="/wiki/Western_Polynesian_tropical_moist_forests" title="Western Polynesian tropical moist forests">Western Polynesian tropical moist forests</a> <a href="/wiki/List_of_terrestrial_ecoregions_(WWF)" title="List of terrestrial ecoregions (WWF)">ecoregion</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-62">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> Most of the original vegetation has been replaced by <a href="/wiki/Coconut" title="Coconut">coconut</a> plantations, some of which have been abandoned and became scrubby forests. The atolls of Tokelau provide habitat for 38 indigenous plant species, over 150 insect species and 10 land crab species. One of the greatest threats to biodiversity is posed by introduced mammalian predators such as the <a href="/wiki/Polynesian_Rat" class="mw-redirect" title="Polynesian Rat">Polynesian Rat</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2011 Tokelau declared its entire <a href="/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone" title="Exclusive economic zone">exclusive economic zone</a> of 319,031&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (123,179&#160;sq&#160;mi) a <a href="/wiki/Shark_sanctuary" title="Shark sanctuary">shark sanctuary</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .mbox-text-span{margin-left:23px!important}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .ambox{margin:0 10%}}</style><table class="box-Update plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Update" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg/42px-Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg.png" decoding="async" width="42" height="34" class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section needs to be <b>updated</b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">June 2017</span>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Tokelau_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Tokelau_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/220px-Tokelau_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Tokelau_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/330px-Tokelau_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Tokelau_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/440px-Tokelau_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Tokelau_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of Tokelau exports, 2019</div></div></div>
<p>According to the US <a href="/wiki/Central_Intelligence_Agency" title="Central Intelligence Agency">Central Intelligence Agency</a>'s <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(PPP)" title="List of countries by GDP (PPP)">list of countries by GDP (PPP)</a> Tokelau has the smallest economy in the world. Tokelau has an annual <a href="/wiki/Purchasing_power" title="Purchasing power">purchasing power</a> of about <a href="/wiki/United_States_dollar" title="United States dollar">US$</a>1,000 (<a href="/wiki/Euro" title="Euro">€</a>674) per capita. The government is almost entirely dependent on <a href="/wiki/Subsidy" title="Subsidy">subsidies</a> from New Zealand. It has annual revenues of less than US$500,000 (€336,995) against expenditures of some US$2.8 million (€1.9 million). The deficit is made up by aid from New Zealand.
</p><p>Tokelau annually exports around US$100,000 (€67,000) of <a href="/wiki/Postage_stamp" title="Postage stamp">stamps</a>, <a href="/wiki/Copra" title="Copra">copra</a> and woven and carved handicrafts and imports over US$300,000 (€202,000) of foodstuffs, building materials, and fuel to, and from, New Zealand. New Zealand also pays directly for the cost of medical and education services. Local industries include small-scale enterprises for <a href="/wiki/Copra" title="Copra">copra</a> production, wood work, plaited craft goods, stamps, coins, and fishing. Agriculture and livestock produces <a href="/wiki/Coconut" title="Coconut">coconuts</a>, copra, <a href="/wiki/Breadfruit" title="Breadfruit">breadfruit</a>, <a href="/wiki/Papaya" title="Papaya">papayas</a>, bananas, <a href="/wiki/Common_fig" class="mw-redirect" title="Common fig">figs</a>, pigs, poultry and a few goats. Many Tokelauans live in New Zealand and support their families in Tokelau through <a href="/wiki/Remittance" title="Remittance">remittances</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Solar_power">Solar power</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Solar power">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The goal of <a href="/wiki/100%25_renewable_energy" title="100% renewable energy">100% renewable electricity</a> was met on 7 November 2012, according to the <a href="/wiki/Foreign_Affairs_Minister_of_New_Zealand" class="mw-redirect" title="Foreign Affairs Minister of New Zealand">Foreign Affairs Minister of New Zealand</a>, <a href="/wiki/Murray_McCully" title="Murray McCully">Murray McCully</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> Previously electricity was generated using diesel generators and was only available about 16&#160;hours/day.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Three <a href="/wiki/Solar_power" title="Solar power">solar power</a> stations with a total generation capacity of 930kWp were installed to provide 100% of current electrical demand from <a href="/wiki/Photovoltaics" title="Photovoltaics">photovoltaics</a>, with lead acid battery backup able to store around 8MWh.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> The first power station was completed in August 2012. In total, 4,032 solar panels are used and 1,344 batteries weighing 250 kilograms (550&#160;lb)&#160;each. The systems are designed to withstand winds of 230&#160;km/h (143&#160;mph).<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> By 2011, Tokelau's electricity was 93% generated by <a href="/wiki/Photovoltaic" class="mw-redirect" title="Photovoltaic">photovoltaics</a>, with the remainder generated from <a href="/wiki/Coconut_oil" title="Coconut oil">coconut oil</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> As of 2019, increased demand and degradation of batteries had led to increased need for backup power.<sup id="cite_ref-offgrid_71-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-offgrid-71">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> Plans were made for an additional 210&#160;kW of PV and close to 2MWh of lithium-ion battery capacity.<sup id="cite_ref-offgrid_71-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-offgrid-71">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul class="gallery mw-gallery-traditional">
		<li class="gallerybox" style="width: 235px"><div style="width: 235px">

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Internet_domain_name">Internet domain name</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Internet domain name">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/.tk" title=".tk">.tk</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:TokelauInternetByAtoll2011.png" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/TokelauInternetByAtoll2011.png/220px-TokelauInternetByAtoll2011.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="136" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/TokelauInternetByAtoll2011.png/330px-TokelauInternetByAtoll2011.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/TokelauInternetByAtoll2011.png/440px-TokelauInternetByAtoll2011.png 2x" data-file-width="584" data-file-height="361" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:TokelauInternetByAtoll2011.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Access to internet in Tokelau, 2011</div></div></div>
<p>Tokelau has increased its GDP by more than 10% through registrations of <a href="/wiki/Domain_name" title="Domain name">domain names</a> under its <a href="/wiki/Top-level_domain" title="Top-level domain">top-level domain</a>, <a href="/wiki/.tk" title=".tk">.tk</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> Registrations can be either free, in which case the user owns only usage rights and not the domain itself, or paid, which grants full rights. Free domains are pointed to Tokelau <a href="/wiki/Name_server" title="Name server">name servers</a>, which redirects the domain via <a href="/wiki/HTML_frames" class="mw-redirect" title="HTML frames">HTML frames</a> to a specified address or to a specified <a href="/wiki/A_record" class="mw-redirect" title="A record">A</a> or <a href="/wiki/NS_record" class="mw-redirect" title="NS record">NS record</a>, and the redirection of up to 250 email addresses to an external address (not at a .tk domain).
</p><p>In September 2003 <a href="/wiki/Fakaofo" title="Fakaofo">Fakaofo</a> became the first part of Tokelau with a high-speed Internet connection. Foundation Tokelau financed the project. Tokelau gives most domain names under its authority away to anyone for free to gain publicity for the territory. This has allowed the nation to gain enhanced telecommunications technologies, such as more computers and Internet access for Tokelauan residents. By 2012, there were about 120 computers, mostly laptops, and 1/6th of the economy consists of income from .tk domain names.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to a 2016 analysis of domain name registration performed by the <a href="/wiki/.uk" title=".uk">.uk</a> registrar <a href="/wiki/Nominet" class="mw-redirect" title="Nominet">Nominet</a> using data from ZookNIC,<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> tk domains are the "world's largest country-code domain ... almost as large as second and third place holders China (<a href="/wiki/.cn" title=".cn">.cn</a>) and Germany (<a href="/wiki/.de" title=".de">.de</a>) combined".<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:LanguagesSpokenTokelau2011.png" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0c/LanguagesSpokenTokelau2011.png/220px-LanguagesSpokenTokelau2011.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="149" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0c/LanguagesSpokenTokelau2011.png/330px-LanguagesSpokenTokelau2011.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0c/LanguagesSpokenTokelau2011.png/440px-LanguagesSpokenTokelau2011.png 2x" data-file-width="509" data-file-height="345" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:LanguagesSpokenTokelau2011.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Language statistics in Tokelau, 2006 and 2011</div></div></div>
<p>According to the 2016 Tokelau Census, Tokelau has a <i><a href="/wiki/De_jure" title="De jure">de jure</a></i> usually resident population of 1,499 people. The census shows a 6.2% increase in the de jure usually resident population between 2011 and 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The nationals of Tokelau are called <a href="/wiki/Tokelauan_people" title="Tokelauan people">Tokelauans</a>, and the major ethnic group is <a href="/wiki/Polynesian_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Polynesian people">Polynesian</a>; it has no recorded minority groups. About 84% of inhabitants are of wholly or partly Tokelauan ethnicity; people of <a href="/wiki/Samoa" title="Samoa">Samoan</a> ethnicity make up 6.7% of the population, and <a href="/wiki/Tuvalu" title="Tuvalu">Tuvaluans</a> 2.8%.<sup id="cite_ref-CIAPAPUANEWGUINEA_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIAPAPUANEWGUINEA-77">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> The main language—spoken by over 90% of inhabitants—is <a href="/wiki/Tokelauan_language" title="Tokelauan language">Tokelauan</a>, but almost 60% also speak English.
</p><p>The less than 1,500 <a href="/wiki/Polynesia#Cultures_of_Polynesia" title="Polynesia">Polynesian</a> inhabitants live in three villages. Their isolation and lack of resources greatly limits economic development and confines agriculture to the subsistence level. The very limited natural resources and overcrowding are contributing to emigration to <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand" title="New Zealand">New Zealand</a> and <a href="/wiki/Samoa" title="Samoa">Samoa</a>. In the 2013 New Zealand census, more than 7,000 people identified as Tokelauan, almost five times as many as live in Tokelau itself.<sup id="cite_ref-tokelau.org.nz_10-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tokelau.org.nz-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> Depletion of <a href="/wiki/Tuna" title="Tuna">tuna</a> has made fishing for food more difficult.
</p><p>A significant proportion (44.9% in 2016) of the population <a href="/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states_and_dependent_territories_by_immigrant_population" title="List of sovereign states and dependent territories by immigrant population">were born overseas</a>, mostly in Samoa (15.3% of total population) and New Zealand (11.5%).<sup id="cite_ref-tokelau.org.nz_10-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tokelau.org.nz-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>While slightly more females than males live on Atafu and Fakaofo, males make up 57% of Nukunonu residents.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> Only 9% of Tokelauans aged 40 or more have never been married.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> One-quarter of the population were born overseas; almost all the rest live on the same atoll they were born on.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> Most households own five or more pigs.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Despite its low income, Tokelau has a life expectancy of 69 years, comparable with other Oceania islands.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Tokelau_Nukuono_Church_20070716.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Tokelau_Nukuono_Church_20070716.jpg/220px-Tokelau_Nukuono_Church_20070716.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Tokelau_Nukuono_Church_20070716.jpg/330px-Tokelau_Nukuono_Church_20070716.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Tokelau_Nukuono_Church_20070716.jpg/440px-Tokelau_Nukuono_Church_20070716.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1920" data-file-height="2560" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Tokelau_Nukuono_Church_20070716.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Catholic Church on Nukunonu in Tokelau</div></div></div>
<p>Tokelau is predominantly <a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christian</a>. On the island of <a href="/wiki/Atafu" title="Atafu">Atafu</a> almost all inhabitants are members of the <a href="/wiki/Congregational_Christian_Church_in_Samoa" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregational Christian Church in Samoa">Congregational Christian Church of Samoa</a> (corresponding to 62% of the total population). On <a href="/wiki/Nukunonu" title="Nukunonu">Nukunonu</a> almost all are <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic">Roman Catholic</a> (corresponding to 34% of the total population). On <a href="/wiki/Fakaofo" title="Fakaofo">Fakaofo</a> both denominations are present with the Congregational Christian Church predominant. 5% of the population follow other religions.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Healthcare_and_education">Healthcare and education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Healthcare and education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Healthcare_in_Tokelau" class="mw-redirect" title="Healthcare in Tokelau">Healthcare in Tokelau</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:TokelauLiteracyByAge2011.png" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/TokelauLiteracyByAge2011.png/220px-TokelauLiteracyByAge2011.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="139" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/TokelauLiteracyByAge2011.png/330px-TokelauLiteracyByAge2011.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/TokelauLiteracyByAge2011.png/440px-TokelauLiteracyByAge2011.png 2x" data-file-width="622" data-file-height="394" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:TokelauLiteracyByAge2011.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Literacy by age in Tokelau, 2011 census</div></div></div>
<p>Each atoll has a school and hospital. The health services have a Director of Health in <a href="/wiki/Apia" title="Apia">Apia</a> and a Chief Clinical Advisor who moves from atoll to atoll as required to assist the doctors attached to each hospital. In 2007 there was not always a doctor on each island and <a href="/wiki/Locum" title="Locum">locums</a> were appointed to fill the gaps.
</p><p>Many Tokelauan youth travel to New Zealand to further their education and Tokelau is most populated around the Christmas season, with students returning home and then heading off for another year of study.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sport">Sport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Sport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Rugby_union_in_Tokelau" title="Rugby union in Tokelau">Rugby union in Tokelau</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rugby_league_in_Tokelau" title="Rugby league in Tokelau">Rugby league in Tokelau</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Tokelau_at_the_Pacific_Games" title="Tokelau at the Pacific Games">Tokelau at the Pacific Games</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Cricket,_Tokelau_Islands,_1966_(17501454372)_(2).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/da/Cricket%2C_Tokelau_Islands%2C_1966_%2817501454372%29_%282%29.jpg/220px-Cricket%2C_Tokelau_Islands%2C_1966_%2817501454372%29_%282%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="166" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/da/Cricket%2C_Tokelau_Islands%2C_1966_%2817501454372%29_%282%29.jpg/330px-Cricket%2C_Tokelau_Islands%2C_1966_%2817501454372%29_%282%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/da/Cricket%2C_Tokelau_Islands%2C_1966_%2817501454372%29_%282%29.jpg/440px-Cricket%2C_Tokelau_Islands%2C_1966_%2817501454372%29_%282%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="900" data-file-height="680" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Cricket,_Tokelau_Islands,_1966_(17501454372)_(2).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Cricket" title="Cricket">Cricket</a> in Tokelau, 1966</div></div></div>
<p>Due to its small size, Tokelau is unaffiliated to most international sports organisations, and rarely takes part in international events. The only significant international competition Tokelau takes part in is the <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Games" title="Pacific Games">Pacific Games</a>. Tokelau won its first-ever gold medals at the <a href="/wiki/2007_Pacific_Games" class="mw-redirect" title="2007 Pacific Games">2007 Pacific Games</a> in <a href="/wiki/Apia" title="Apia">Apia</a>, winning a total of five medals (three gold, a silver and a bronze), all in <a href="/wiki/Lawn_bowls" class="mw-redirect" title="Lawn bowls">lawn bowls</a>, and finishing 12th (out of 22) on the overall medal table. This included two gold medals for <a href="/wiki/Violina_Linda_Pedro" title="Violina Linda Pedro">Violina Linda Pedro</a> (in the women's pairs and the women's singles), making her Tokelau's most successful individual athlete to date.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In October 2010, <a href="/wiki/Table_tennis" title="Table tennis">table tennis</a> became "the first sport in Tokelau to be granted membership at a Continental or World level", when the <a href="/wiki/Tokelau_Table_Tennis_Association" title="Tokelau Table Tennis Association">Tokelau Table Tennis Association</a> was formally established and became the 23rd member of the <a href="/wiki/Oceania_Table_Tennis_Federation" title="Oceania Table Tennis Federation">Oceania Table Tennis Federation</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-ittf.com_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ittf.com-85">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Tokelau was due to take part, for the first time, in the <a href="/wiki/2010_Commonwealth_Games" title="2010 Commonwealth Games">2010 Commonwealth Games</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Delhi" title="Delhi">Delhi</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> but, for unknown reasons, ultimately did not do so.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Tokelau has a National Sports Federation, and a significant sporting event is the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau_Games&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Tokelau Games (page does not exist)">Tokelau Games</a>, which are held yearly. When they are held, "all of Tokelau virtually stands still", as "[i]n excess of 50% of the population take part and all work and school stops at the time". The 2010 Games included competitions in <a href="/wiki/Rugby_sevens" title="Rugby sevens">rugby sevens</a>, <a href="/wiki/Netball" title="Netball">netball</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kilikiti" title="Kilikiti">kilikiti</a>, alongside "a cultural evening [...] where each atoll showcases their traditional songs and dances".<sup id="cite_ref-ittf.com_85-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ittf.com-85">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Netball" title="Netball">Netball</a> is thought to have been introduced to Tokelau by the British, but became more popular when New Zealand's government took over the territory. The sport is often played during inter-island sport competitions, alongside other sports like <a href="/wiki/Rugby_league" title="Rugby league">rugby league</a> and <a href="/wiki/Volleyball" title="Volleyball">volleyball</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In Tokelau, there are two levels to the <a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">football</a> league. From <a href="/wiki/Fale,_Tokelau" title="Fale, Tokelau">Fale</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fakaofo" title="Fakaofo">Fakaofo</a>, two of the best clubs are <a href="/w/index.php?title=Hakava_Club&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Hakava Club (page does not exist)">Hakava Club</a> and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Matalele_Club&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Matalele Club (page does not exist)">Matalele Club</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-rsssf_89-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rsssf-89">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Communication_and_transportation">Communication and transportation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Tokelau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Communication and transportation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Tokelau_barge.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Tokelau_barge.JPG/220px-Tokelau_barge.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Tokelau_barge.JPG/330px-Tokelau_barge.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Tokelau_barge.JPG/440px-Tokelau_barge.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2560" data-file-height="1920" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Tokelau_barge.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A barge leaves the landing ramp in <a href="/wiki/Nukunonu" title="Nukunonu">Nukunonu</a> to collect cargo and passengers from the <a href="/wiki/MV_Tokelau" title="MV Tokelau">MV <i>Tokelau</i></a></div></div></div>
<p>Tokelau has a radio telephone service between the islands and to <a href="/wiki/Samoa" title="Samoa">Samoa</a>. In 1997, a government-regulated telephone service (TeleTok) with three satellite earth stations was established. Each atoll has a radio-broadcast station that broadcasts shipping and weather reports and every household has a radio or access to one. News is disseminated through the government <a href="/wiki/Newsletter" title="Newsletter">newsletter</a> <i><a href="/wiki/Te_Vakai" title="Te Vakai">Te Vakai</a></i>.
</p><p>Tokelau has the <a href="/wiki/International_calling_code" class="mw-redirect" title="International calling code">international calling code</a> of 690, and has had five-digit telephone numbers from November 2015 (the existing four-digit numbers were prefixed by the digit "2").<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Tokelau is served by the MV <i>Mataliki</i>, delivered new in 2016 as a replacement of the smaller <i>MV Tokelau</i> and jointly managed by the Tokelau Transport Department and the company Transport and Marine. The vessel, which has a capacity of 60 passengers on international cruises and 120 for transport between the atolls of Tokelau, operates fortnightly between Tokelau and <a href="/wiki/Apia" title="Apia">Apia</a>, with the trip taking a little over a day.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> A dedicated cargo vessel, the <i>MV Kalopaga</i>, will enter service in 2018 and replace chartered freight vessels.<sup id="cite_ref-Radio_NZ_92-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Radio_NZ-92">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ships load and unload cargo by motoring up to the down-wind (<a href="/wiki/Leeward" class="mw-redirect" title="Leeward">leeward</a>) side of the islet where the people live and maintaining station, by intermittent use of engines, close to the reef edge so that a landing barge can be motored out to transfer cargo to or from the shore. On returning to shore, the barge negotiates a narrow channel through the reef to the beach. Usually this landing is subject to ocean swell and beaching requires considerable skill and, often, <a href="/wiki/Coral" title="Coral">coral</a> abrasions to bodies. When bad weather prevents the barge making the trip, the ship stands off to wait for suitable weather or goes off to one of the other atolls to attempt to load or unload its passengers or cargo, or both.
</p><p>There is no airport in Tokelau, so boats are the main means of travel and transport. Some <a href="/wiki/Seaplanes" class="mw-redirect" title="Seaplanes">seaplanes</a> and <a href="/wiki/Amphibious_aircraft" title="Amphibious aircraft">amphibious aircraft</a> are able to land in the island's lagoons.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> An airstrip was considered by the New Zealand Government in 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> In 2016, plans to link the atolls with Samoa by helicopter had to be abandoned because of high costs, leading in the following years to renewed calls to the New Zealand government for help with establishing air services.<sup id="cite_ref-Radio_NZ_92-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Radio_NZ-92">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<li id="cite_note-Aliens_and_citizens-47"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Aliens_and_citizens_47-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFGreen2012" class="citation encyclopaedia cs1">Green, David (13 July 2012). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.teara.govt.nz/en/citizenship/3">"Citizenship&#160;– Aliens and citizens"</a>. <i>Te Ara&#160;– the Encyclopedia of New Zealand</i>. Ministry for Culture and Heritage<span class="reference-accessdate">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">27 August</span> 2016</span>.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=bookitem&amp;rft.atitle=Citizenship+%E2%80%93+Aliens+and+citizens&amp;rft.btitle=Te+Ara+%E2%80%93+the+Encyclopedia+of+New+Zealand&amp;rft.pub=Ministry+for+Culture+and+Heritage&amp;rft.date=2012-07-13&amp;rft.aulast=Green&amp;rft.aufirst=David&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.teara.govt.nz%2Fen%2Fcitizenship%2F3&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ATokelau" class="Z3988"></span></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-48"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-48">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite class="citation web cs1"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20140220023040/http://tokelau.org.nz/">"Official site for the Tokelau Council of Ongoing Government"</a>. Archived from <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.tokelau.org.nz/">the original</a> on 20 February 2014<span class="reference-accessdate">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">4 November</span> 2007</span>. <q>The legislation and judicial systems are based on the Tokelau Act, 1948, and its amendments. A major law reform project is continuing; its purpose is to ensure that Tokelau has a coherent body of law which responds to current needs and gives due recognition to local custom. Unless it is expressly extended to Tokelau, New Zealand statute law does not apply to the territory. In practice, no New Zealand legislation is extended to Tokelau without Tokelauan consent. The villages have the statutory power to enact their own laws covering village affairs. International covenants on economic, social and cultural rights and civil and political rights, ratified by New Zealand in December 1978, apply in Tokelau. Civil and criminal jurisdiction is exercised by commissioners and the New Zealand high court.</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.btitle=Official+site+for+the+Tokelau+Council+of+Ongoing+Government&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.tokelau.org.nz%2F&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ATokelau" class="Z3988"></span></span>
</li>

<li>13.&#160;<a href="/wiki/League_of_Nations_mandate" title="League of Nations mandate">League of Nations mandate</a>. British Cameroons is now part of <i><a href="/wiki/Cameroon" title="Cameroon">Cameroon</a></i>, while Tanganyika is part of <i><a href="/wiki/Tanzania" title="Tanzania">Tanzania</a></i>.</li>
<li>14.&#160;<a href="/wiki/Responsible_government" title="Responsible government">Self-governing</a> Southern Rhodesia <a href="/wiki/Rhodesia%27s_Unilateral_Declaration_of_Independence" title="Rhodesia&#39;s Unilateral Declaration of Independence">unilaterally declared independence</a> in 1965 (as <a href="/wiki/Rhodesia" title="Rhodesia">Rhodesia</a>) and continued as an <a href="/wiki/Diplomatic_recognition" title="Diplomatic recognition">unrecognised</a> state until the 1979 <a href="/wiki/Lancaster_House_Agreement" title="Lancaster House Agreement">Lancaster House Agreement</a>. After recognised independence in 1980, Zimbabwe was a member of the Commonwealth until it withdrew in 2003.</li>
<li>15. Now <i><a href="/wiki/Zambia" title="Zambia">Zambia</a></i>.</li></ul>
